# Manual test tracker

Where every one of the 464 tests stands. One row per test, updated as you run it.

This is a **recording** page, like [Planning](planning.md) — you need it when you start a
run and when you finish one, not while executing. Keep [the script](../manual-tests.md),
[Common procedures](common.md) and one area file open for that.

## Status

| Status | Means | What to do |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Active** | Nobody knows yet. Either never run, or run and the environment could not answer. | Run it. |
| **Testing** | A run is in progress right now. | Finish it, then set Pass or Fail. |
| **Pass** | Every *Pass if* line held and no *Fail if* line was observed. | Nothing. |
| **Fail** | A *Fail if* line was observed, or a *Pass if* line was not. | Open an issue and put its number in the Issue column. |

Every row starts **Active**. Set it to **Testing** when you pick the test up, so a second
tester does not start the same one, and to **Pass** or **Fail** when you conclude.

### Blocked and Inconclusive come back here as Active

[§0.5](../manual-tests.md#05-how-to-conclude-a-result) gives a run four possible outcomes:
Blocked, Inconclusive, Fail and Pass. Two of those have no status of their own here, on
purpose — **Blocked** and **Inconclusive** both mean the test is still unanswered, which is
what **Active** means. Record the outcome by leaving the row Active and writing the reason
in Notes: `Inconclusive: no sqlite3`, or `Blocked by SHELLNAV-01`. A row is only Pass or
Fail when the test actually reached a verdict.

That keeps the tracker answering one question — what still needs running — instead of two.

## Failures are tracked as Gitea issues

A **Fail** gets an issue. The result in this table is the index; the issue carries what you
saw, quoted verbatim per [§0.5](../manual-tests.md#05-how-to-conclude-a-result) rule 2, and
the implication the *Fail if* line gives.

Issues live on this repo's Gitea instance under `/emre/Huddle/issues`. The host is
deliberately not written here — `.gitleaks.toml`'s `internal-mdns-host` rule fails the
`secret-scan` job on any `*.local` name in a tracked file, and `gitleaks dir .` scans the
whole tree, so one hardcoded hostname would break CI on somebody else's unrelated commit.
Build the URL from the remote instead, the way [GiteaOperations.md](../../../agents/GiteaOperations.md)
does everywhere else:

```powershell
$base = (git remote get-url origin) -replace '\.git$', ''
Start-Process "$base/issues"          # opens the issue list
```

Put the number in the Issue column as `#123`, linked the same way if you like. Before
opening one, check [§0.6](../manual-tests.md#06-not-a-defect) — some behaviour that looks
broken is a documented decision, and those are a Pass.

Title an issue with the test id first, so the tracker and the issue list line up:
`SHELLNAV-01: app.css href has no fingerprint and 404s`.

Sidebar children in order: new-chat, room-list (echo, alpha), sidebar-link `Teammates`, sidebar-link `Settings`. Pasting the URL into a second tab opened the same Room with the same h1 and the same transcript (`marker`). The open Room's entry carried class `active` with background rgb(47,61,87) against rgba(0,0,0,0) for the other. The JSONL line carried `"text":"line one\nline two"` with the literal backslash-n escape, so the newline really is stored. HARNESS NOTE: the synthetic shift+Enter does not perform the browser's default newline INSERTION, so the second line was entered as text; the app-side halves - does not send, newline preserved - are what this proves. |

Wire oracle on the same envelope: Emily mentioned=true, echo mentioned=false, mentions=['Emily'] on both, members=3. MINTED ROOM: with two Personas, '@Ana create a new room containing yourself and Ben' logged 'Created room ''01a0a31b52067b3988219592d7f1a8a0'' (Ana, Ben) with 3 members.', the rooms table gained the row, and that Room took 2 fresh agent replies of its own while the first Room was already at its cap - the documented gap, not filed. TOKEN BUDGET: reached only by the Agent-to-Agent bounce. Log: 'Persona ''Ana'' has spent its token budget of 2000 and is taking no more turns until a human speaks to it.' The Room strip read 'Ana is Degraded: The per-Persona token Budget of 2000 is spent; no more Turns until a Human speaks.' (and the same for Ben); both tiles read Degraded with agent-dot degraded, and Ana's card carried that reason line under the status. After '@Ana reply with just the word ok' she answered 'ok', the strip cleared and both tiles returned to Online. Two script defects corrected: one Persona can never mint a Room (CreateRoomForAsync short-circuits a single agent to EnsureRoomForAsync), and a Room Budget of 2 stops the bounce before the token check ever runs. |

| [TEAMMATESLIBRARY-40](teammates-library.md#teammateslibrary-40-degraded-with-the-persistence-reason-and-the-two-things-that-must-not-badge) | 💰 | Active | | Inconclusive - NOT EXECUTED, per the test's own Inconclusive clause. The Degraded half needs three consecutive GENUINE Turn failures, and this provider cannot be interrupted mid-Turn by any non-destructive means available here: STREAMINGTURN-29 established that pointing ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL at a dead port and then at a local endpoint answering 401 both make the agent SDK retry and hang rather than fail the Turn (the endpoint was confirmed reached via Get-NetTCPConnection). Disabling the network adapter or revoking the claude CLI login are destructive to this machine and were not attempted, so the failures never land as failures and no money was spent here. The two negatives are already verified on this exact surface by earlier tests and were not re-bought: STREAMINGTURN-25 showed the /teammates tile and card staying Online with a green dot and no reason line through THREE consecutive Human-clicked Stops, and REPLYGATEBUDGET-07 showed /teammates not marking an Agent Degraded when its Room Budget was spent. |

Cost confirmed first: card read Model `Haiku`; Effort read `Model default` rather than `low` because Haiku advertises no effort ladder at all on this adapter (see TEAMMATECARD-41/47), so there is no effort level to raise and the cost is Haiku's own default. Guards in place: Team__AgentMessageBudget=4, Team__Acp__TokenBudget=200000, no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set in any scope, TEAM_E2E unset. BEFORE the edit: `Remember the codeword is PELICAN.` -> `I've got it - codeword noted as PELICAN.`; `What is the codeword?` -> `The codeword is PELICAN.` AFTER changing one word of the Persona body (`You are Live.` -> `You are Alive.`) and saving: badge `Online` -> `Starting` at 616ms -> `Online` at 2238ms, and the adapter process was replaced, pid 91408 -> 22040. The same question then returned `I don't have context for what codeword you're referring to. Could you provide more details?` - the session had forgotten, so the card's promise holds. Three Turns spent. METHOD NOTE, important for anyone re-running this: on the FIRST Turn the Live agent used its Write tool to persist the codeword to a file OUTSIDE its Work Dir - filed as issue #22 and said `The codeword PELICAN is saved to memory`. That file was removed before the edit; had it been left, the restarted session could have read it back and answered PELICAN, which would have produced a false Fail on a test about session memory. |

| [MODELEFFORT-11](model-effort.md#modeleffort-11-edit-preselects-the-stored-model-and-effort-and-keeps-them-when-the-catalog-lands-late) |  | Fail | [#23](http://gitea-host.example:3000/emre/Huddle/issues/23) | The Model select LOSES the stored model when the real catalog lands. Reproduced twice from a cold restart, deterministically. Stored: persona_models `Probe One \| sonnet`, persona_efforts `Probe One \| low`. While the Model hint read `Reading the models this agent offers…` (185ms) the select correctly showed the stored value as a synthesised entry - value `sonnet`, label rendered as the raw id `sonnet` exactly as the first pass line predicts. When the catalog arrived 9.3s later and replaced the option list, the Model select jumped to value `default`, label `Default (recommended)` - NOT `Sonnet`, which is present in the list as `sonnet::Sonnet`. That breaks the second pass line. The Effort select behaved correctly throughout, holding `low` before and after. NOT the catastrophic consequence the Fail-if describes, and this is the important qualifier: the DATABASE SURVIVED BOTH SAVES. A no-op Save left `sonnet`/`low` untouched, and a real Save (Effort low -> medium made from the mis-displayed state) wrote `medium` while LEAVING model at `sonnet`; the resulting View card read `Sonnet` / `Medium` correctly. So the `<select>`'s DOM selection is out of sync with Blazor's bound field, not the bound field itself - display-only, but it tells the user their teammate is on `Default (recommended)` when it is on Sonnet. DISCRIMINATOR: with a WARM catalog the same Edit card is correct - it renders `sonnet`/`Sonnet` within 66ms and never shows `default`. The fault is specific to the option list being replaced late. |

Saving without changing anything left both rows exactly as they were - still `claude-nonexistent-9` and `vintage` - and the card returned to View mode showing both raw values. IMPORTANT DISCRIMINATOR for issue #23: here the synthesised entry KEEPS its selection when the real catalog lands, whereas in MODELEFFORT-11 a stored value that also exists in the catalog (`sonnet`) loses it. So #23 is specifically about the synthesised option being removed once the real list contains an equivalent, with the DOM selection then falling to `default` - not about synthesised options in general. Posted as a comment on #23. |

Across the full 45 seconds the 100ms watcher recorded exactly one rise - 0 -> 1 (pid 62976) at 22:53:12.453, back to 0 at 22:53:17.270 - PEAK 1, never 2. Both tabs ended with the identical 6-entry Model list (`::Use the agent's default, default, opus[1m], claude-fable-5-1[1m], sonnet, haiku`) and the identical 6-entry Effort list, both hints settled to the normal wording, neither stuck on `Reading…`. The app log has zero error-level lines and no stack trace, and #blazor-error-ui was computed display:none in BOTH tabs. No cross-circuit bleed - the two Effort lists matched because both selects were on the same blank model, not because one leaked. |

With the adapter renamed aside to node_modules.off and the app NOT restarted, re-opening the card returned a list IDENTICAL to step 1, the watcher stayed at 3 lines (no new rise at all), and no probe spawned - the successful catalog is cached for the app run exactly as documented. After restarting with the adapter still aside, the Model select contained ONLY `::Use the agent's default` and the Model hint read exactly `This agent advertises no models, so it will use its own default.` - so the list genuinely comes from the adapter and is not hardcoded or persisted, which is the serious failure this test exists to expose. Restoring node_modules and re-probing brought the real 6-entry list back. The documented staleness is recorded here, not filed. |

PART B (authentication) INCONCLUSIVE, as the test's own Inconclusive clause directs: there is no safe way to put the Claude CLI into an unauthenticated state on this machine without disturbing the user's real login, and the test says to record it rather than improvise with credentials. PART C (timeout) NOT OBSERVED: no probe hung during any test in this area - every one settled in 3-11 seconds - so there is nothing to report, which is the expected outcome. Creating `Probe Migrate` with Sonnet + Low wrote straight into the freshly created tables - `Probe Migrate \| sonnet` and `Probe Migrate \| low` - so the stores are writing into them, not just creating them. The immediate SECOND click found no button at all, so only one restart ran - stronger than `does nothing`. NOT a no-op where it matters most: the status reached `Online`, the adapter count returned to 10 with a NEW pid 77724 while the killed 73352 stayed dead, the log gained 30 lines including a fresh 9-line `[agent stderr]` burst, and zero `failed to start` lines. |

The two answers name DIFFERENT models, so the picker's value reaches session/new rather than stopping at the database. EFFORT SELF-REPORT INCONCLUSIVE, exactly as the test directs: the model said `thinking effort is not something I have visibility into from inside the session - that's a harness-level setting`, so the absent effort-catalog warning is the oracle for that half. DEVIATION: step 9 asks to change Effort Low -> Medium, but the step-6 model switch clears the effort (MODELEFFORT-07), so the change made was blank -> Medium on Sonnet. |

METHOD NOTE, the trap the Inconclusive clause warns about: passing a name containing a space through Start-Process -ArgumentList as an array silently split it - the script echoed `as agent 'bad'` and `as agent 'Emily'`, connecting to a mangled pipe name. | [HOOKSSETTINGS-08](hooks-settings.md#hookssettings-08-textarea-height-tracks-the-line-count-and-clamps-at-14-rows) |  | Pass |  | Measured the `rows` attribute and the rendered height after every single newline rather than by eye. THE CLAMP HOLDS AT EXACTLY 14: newlines 13 through 18 left rows at `14` and the height frozen at 290px, while scrollHeight kept climbing (288 -> 308 -> 327 -> 346 -> 366 -> 385) against a fixed clientHeight of 288 - i.e. the box scrolls internally instead of growing. THE RESIZE GRIP IS PRESENT: computed `resize: vertical`. NO SHIPPED DEFAULT IS ANYWHERE NEAR THE CAP: across all 22 fields the tallest is `Tools` at 7 lines / rows=8, and none renders at 14, matching the NOTE exactly. ONE ARITHMETIC DISCREPANCY IN THE TEST'S PROSE, NOT IN THE APP: the pass conditions say the first THIRTEEN Enter presses each add a row and that growth stops after the thirteenth, yet also that rows then reads 14 - those three statements cannot all hold together, since 2 + 13 = 15. The implementation is `Math.Clamp(lineCount + 1, 2, 14)` (HookFieldFactory.RowsFor), so rows = lines + 1 and the 14-row cap is reached at 12 newlines. The cap VALUE the test names, the starting height, the internal scrolling and the grip all match; only the press count in the prose is off by one. Passed on that basis rather than filing a defect against correct behaviour. |

| [HOOKSSETTINGS-10](hooks-settings.md#hookssettings-10-modified-and-unsaved-are-independent-flags-walk-all-three-combinations) |  | Active |  | INCONCLUSIVE ON STEP 7, EXACTLY AS THE TEST'S OWN INCONCLUSIVE CLAUSE PRESCRIBES - and the cause is located precisely, so it can be judged at HOOKSSETTINGS-35 where it belongs. WHAT PASSED. Step 2-3: after saving `Chat rules` with ` ZZZ` appended, the field shows `Modified` ONLY, no `Unsaved`, and Save goes disabled. Step 4: hooks.json holds EXACTLY ONE top-level key, `systemPrompt.chatRules`, whose value ends in ` ZZZ`. Step 5-6: typing ` QQQ` brings BOTH `Modified` and `Unsaved` back. Step 9-10: setting the text back to exactly the stored override makes `Unsaved` DISAPPEAR while `Modified` REMAINS and `Save` goes DISABLED again - so the two flags are genuinely independent and are not collapsed into one. WHAT COULD NOT BE JUDGED. Step 7-8 requires typing the shipped default back byte-for-byte and expects `Modified` to clear. Typing it back does NOT clear it, but clicking that field's `Reset` DOES - which is the exact split the Inconclusive clause routes to HOOKSSETTINGS-35. THIS IS NOT A PASTE ARTEFACT: the string set into the textarea was captured from that same textarea before any edit, and after Reset the textarea's value compares EQUAL to it in JavaScript (same 387 characters, no CR in either). Proved further with a minimal probe: from the post-Reset state (Modified off) typing one `Q` and deleting it returns the value to a string that tests equal to the default, yet `Modified` stays ON and the field `Reset` stays enabled. ROOT CAUSE FOUND: src/Huddle.App/Hooks/HookCatalog.cs is a CRLF file, so `definition.Default` for a multi-line hook contains \r\n, while a browser normalises a textarea's value to \n - and HookFieldFactory compares them with StringComparison.Ordinal (`IsModified: !string.Equals(value, definition.Default, Ordinal)`). So any value that has passed through the textarea can never equal the default, while the Reset button assigns `definition.Default` directly and therefore can. (hooks.default.json is a separate copy and holds LF, which is why the 387-character comparison looked identical.) Left Active per the clause; the defect itself is judged at HOOKSSETTINGS-35. |

Browser Back to /settings/appearance and Forward to /settings/hooks behaved identically - the edit and both badges survived, still one navigation entry. A RELOAD DESTROYS IT: after F5 both fields show their shipped defaults with NO badges - ` KEEPME` and ` KEEPME2` are gone. IN-APP NAVIGATION DESTROYS IT TOO: typing ` LOSEME` then clicking `Teammates` and back to `Settings` leaves the field at its default with no badges. THE THEME CHANGE FORCES A FULL DOCUMENT LOAD, which is the case this test exists for: with ` LOSEME2` pending, selecting `Dark` on the Appearance tab wiped a JS marker set immediately before (window.__marker gone), so the whole document reloaded rather than the pane updating - and the theme really applied, body background rgb(27,27,31) with themes/huddle-dark.css now in <head>. Returning to the Hooks tab shows `Room label` back at `[Room: {{roomName}} (id: {{roomId}})]` with no badges, so ` LOSEME2` is gone. NO UNSAVED-CHANGES PROMPT APPEARED AT ANY POINT: a MutationObserver watching the body for `unsaved changes`/`are you sure`/`discard` caught nothing across all three destructive gestures. That absence is the documented designed behaviour and is recorded as such, not filed. |

Clicking it shows the SAME inline confirm pair, `Yes, reset everything` / `Cancel`, on that tab. CONFIRMING CHANGED NEITHER FILE: appearance.json is still absent (not created, not written) and hooks.json is byte-identical by hash, still holding `turn.roomLabel` with ` MARKER` - which follows from there being no Save button on this pane to commit with. THE RESET WAS STAGED AND SURVIVED THE TAB SWITCH: back on the Hooks tab, `Room label` reads its shipped default `[Room: {{roomName}} (id: {{roomId}})]` and carries an `Unsaved` badge, with navigation entries still 1. UX FINDING, RECORDED AS THE NOTE DIRECTS RATHER THAN FILED AS A BUG: a destructive control labelled only `Reset all to defaults` is rendered on a tab that shows nothing but theme settings, it silently targets a different feature, there is no visible list of what it will affect, and the tab it sits on has no Save button - so the staged destruction is invisible until the user returns to Hooks. (Its danger styling is also not rendering - see HOOKSSETTINGS-16 and issue #28 - which makes the control even less distinguishable.) |

A SAVE IN A REPAINTS B WITHOUT A RELOAD: after tab A appended ` FROMA` to `Chat rules` and saved, tab B - untouched, with a JS marker still alive and still 1 navigation entry - showed the new text ending ` it. FROMA` and a `Modified` badge, within the 5-second window. So the repaint merges rather than clobbers. NO JAVASCRIPT ERRORS in either tab's console during the test (the only console errors in the buffer are timestamped before the app restart that preceded it, from the seconds when the server was down). NO SERVER-SIDE LEAK: the app log stayed at 14 lines with zero matches for exception/error/unhandled/warn/fail and an empty stderr - across closing tab B entirely AND five further open/close cycles of /settings in fresh tabs. So the component is unsubscribing from the singleton store on dispose. |
| [HOOKSSETTINGS-29](hooks-settings.md#hookssettings-29-a-hand-edit-to-hooksjson-reaches-the-open-page-within-about-a-second-with-no-restart-and-no-refresh) |  | Pass |  | THE FILE WATCHER IS ALIVE AND FAST. Writing `{"turn.roomLabel": "[R {{roomId}}]"}` to App_Data\hooks.json from outside the browser, with the page open and untouched, changed the `Room label` textarea to `[R {{roomId}}]` **515 ms** after the write - measured by correlating the file-write epoch with a MutationObserver timestamp in the page, not estimated. No page reload and no app restart: a JS marker set before the write survived and performance navigation entries stayed at 1. A `Modified` badge appeared on that field and `Reset all to defaults` became enabled. THREE RAPID SAVES SETTLE ON THE LAST VALUE: three writes 9 ms apart left the field on `[RRRR {{roomId}}]`; repeated twice more with `[S/SS/SSS]` and `[T/TT/TTT]` bursts, each settling on the final value, still with no reload. NO FIELD EVER BLANKED: a watcher checking every .hooks-field textarea for empty content recorded ZERO blank samples during the first edit, and a direct value sampler recorded ZERO blanks across a further 9 seconds spanning a burst - so the mid-write race is being retried rather than momentarily reverting every hook. THE SERVER CONSOLE IS CLEAN: 14 lines, zero matches for warn/error/exception, and specifically NO `HookStore's FileSystemWatcher reported an error…` line, so the environmental condition the Inconclusive clause names did not occur. ONE SUB-CONDITION COULD NOT BE MEASURED AND IS REPORTED RATHER THAN GUESSED: the exact number of repaints during a burst. Blazor updates a textarea's `value` PROPERTY rather than its attribute or child text, so a MutationObserver sees nothing (0 mutations recorded across a burst that demonstrably changed the value), and a high-frequency MessageChannel sampler starves Blazor's own message pump, so it observes only the end state. What is established is that the burst produced no blanking, no reload, and the correct final value - a visible repaint storm would have shown up in the blank/value watchers as intermediate values, and none appeared. |

(The sub-second figure is not claimed for this gesture specifically; HOOKSSETTINGS-29 measured the same watcher at 515ms for a write.) EVERY `Modified` BADGE DISAPPEARED across all 22 fields - the badge sweep returns an empty list - and `Reset all to defaults` went DISABLED, so the app really is resolving from the catalog again rather than holding stale text. THE APP NEVER RECREATES THE FILE: hooks.json was absent immediately after the delete and still absent after a 35-second poll checking once a second, so no file of defaults was written to silently pin today's wording. NO ERROR SURFACE: #blazor-error-ui computes display:none, no `unhandled error`/`could not` text anywhere on the page, and the server log stayed at 14 lines with ZERO matches for warn/error/exception - a delete is tolerated exactly like an empty file. |
| [HOOKSSETTINGS-31](hooks-settings.md#hookssettings-31-silent-a-malformed-hooksjson-edited-while-running-changes-nothing-on-screen-and-says-nothing-check-the-log) |  | Pass |  | THE HEADLINE SILENT FAILURE BEHAVES EXACTLY AS DOCUMENTED. With ` GOODVALUE` saved into `Room label`, the final closing brace was removed from hooks.json while the app ran. OVER A FULL 11-SECOND WATCH NOTHING CHANGED ON SCREEN: `Room label` still reads `[Room: {{roomName}} (id: {{roomId}})] GOODVALUE` - the last-good text - still with its `Modified` badge; the JS marker survived and navigation entries stayed at 1. THERE IS NO ERROR ANYWHERE ON THE PAGE: a regex over the body text for `could not`/`unhandled`/`error`/`cannot` returns ZERO matches, and #blazor-error-ui computes display:none - so no red line, no banner and no yellow strip. The circuit is alive. THE WARNING IS IN THE CONSOLE, WITH THE EXACT WORDING: `warn: Agency.Huddle.App.Hooks.HookStore[0]` followed by `Could not parse hook overrides file 'E:\Repos\Huddle\src\Huddle.App\App_Data\hooks.json' after a filesystem change, even after retrying; keeping the previously resolved hook text rather than reverting every hook to its catalog default over what may be a mid-write race.` with the JsonException beneath it naming the open object and the exact line. So the failure IS diagnosable - which is the part that would have been a defect if missing. RESTORING THE BRACE needed no further action: the page continued to show the override with the `Modified` badge intact. The on-screen silence is recorded as documented design, not filed. |
| [HOOKSSETTINGS-32](hooks-settings.md#hookssettings-32-silent-a-malformed-hooksjson-at-startup-falls-back-to-defaults-wholesale-with-no-ui-clue-and-the-file-left-intact) |  | Pass |  | BOTH KINDS OF CORRUPTION TESTED, both at STARTUP. (a) `{"turn.roomLabel": 42}` - a non-string value. (b) `{"turn.roomLabel": ` - truncated with no value and no closing brace. THE APP STARTED NORMALLY BOTH TIMES: /settings returned 200 in each case, with no crash and no startup exception. THE WARNING IS PRINTED BOTH TIMES with the exact wording `Could not parse hook overrides file 'E:\Repos\Huddle\src\Huddle.App\App_Data\hooks.json'; falling back to defaults for every hook.` - so the one available diagnosis exists. ALL 22 FIELDS SHOW THEIR SHIPPED DEFAULTS WITH NO BADGES in both cases - the badge sweep returns an empty list and `Room label` reads `[Room: {{roomName}} (id: {{roomId}})]`, exactly as if the file were absent. NO ON-SCREEN CLUE: #blazor-error-ui is display:none and, after excluding the page's own legitimate `Overrides are stored at …hooks.json` sentence, the body text contains no mention of the file being unreadable - documented design, recorded rather than filed. THE FILE IS NEVER TOUCHED: after each startup hooks.json is byte-identical by SHA256 to the scratch copy taken before launch, still holding `{"turn.roomLabel": 42}` and `{"turn.roomLabel": ` respectively - neither overwritten nor deleted. FOLLOW-ON HAZARD CONFIRMED PRESENT, as the NOTE asks: because the fallback reads the corrupt file as empty, a subsequent Save from the browser would write only the new edits and the corrupt content would be silently gone. That is a consequence of the fallback rather than a separate defect, but a tester could lose data that way. |

Appearance tab, Themes, Tokens and overrides — [area file](appearance-theme.md)

| [APPEARANCETHEME-01](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-01-the-appearance-tab-is-on-the-settings-rail-and-is-reachable-by-its-own-route) |  | Pass |  | /settings shows h1 `Settings` and a rail of EXACTLY two buttons, `Hooks` then `Appearance`, with `Hooks` carrying `settings-tab-active` and the Hooks panel rendered. Clicking `Appearance` changed the address bar to http://localhost:5100/settings/appearance and moved the active class to `Appearance` - it navigates rather than flipping local state, so the tab is bookmarkable. Typing /settings/appearance directly lands on the same panel with `Appearance` active. /settings/nonsense renders the full, styled HOOKS panel (4530 characters of main content, Hooks intro present, Hooks marked active) - not blank, not a 404, not an error - so the fallback branch is intact, and the same holds for /settings with no segment. Zero exception/fail lines in T-A across all four navigations (log still 14 lines). #blazor-error-ui display:none throughout. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-02](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-02-the-appearance-tab-shows-its-own-prose-and-the-real-absolute-override-path-and-none-of-the-hooks-tabs-prose) |  | Pass |  | Appearance panel's first paragraph matches word for word: `Pick a theme, or leave it on System to follow your device's own light or dark setting.` The paragraph below the Theme dropdown matches word for word too, including the trailing sentence `The file does not exist until you save a theme choice here, so it being absent is expected, not a bug.` The three monospace <code> elements read exactly `E:\Repos\Huddle\src\Huddle.App\App_Data\appearance.json`, `--font-chat` and `--surface-base` - no leading `@`, no doubled `@@`, no stray escape. The path is ABSOLUTE and ends in \src\Huddle.App\App_Data\appearance.json, and it matches the machine's real data directory, so it is the authority for tests 10-21. The Appearance panel contains NEITHER the Hooks intro sentence (`A hook is one piece of wording this application sends to a model`) NOR any mention of `hooks.json` - both checks returned false - while the Hooks panel does carry the Hooks intro. The shipped regression (shared paragraphs above the rail) has not returned. |

The visible labels are the friendly names `System`/`Light`/`Dark`, not the raw ids `huddle-light`/`huddle-dark` - the ids live only in the option values, so nothing leaks into the display. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-04](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-04-choosing-a-theme-stores-its-id-and-forces-a-full-document-load-not-an-in-place-repaint) |  | Pass |  | Selecting `Dark` forced a REAL full document load, proved by a JS marker set immediately before the change being gone afterwards (window.__marker survived=false) while performance navigation type read `navigate`. After the load the URL is still /settings/appearance with `Appearance` carrying settings-tab-active and the dropdown showing value `huddle-dark` / label `Dark`. The application is now dark - body background rgb(27,27,31) - and <head> gained a fourth stylesheet `themes/huddle-dark.css`. The file was written and reads EXACTLY, across three lines:\n{\n  "theme": "huddle-dark"\n}\n30 bytes. The stored value is the ID `huddle-dark`, NOT the label `Dark`; the file is indented rather than one line and contains no \\uXXXX escapes. The full page load is BY DESIGN and is not filed. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-05](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-05-the-three-cascade-layers-appear-in-head-in-exactly-the-right-order-and-every-stylesheet-is-actually-served) |  | Pass |  | Read from the RAW server HTML (Invoke-WebRequest), not the Blazor DOM, so this is what the server actually emits. With theme huddle-dark + an --accent override, <head> holds in this exact order: theme.ce2n94aiaf.css, app.okpt0txbqy.css, Huddle.App.j8c8gsv1bg.styles.css, then `<link rel="stylesheet" href="themes/huddle-dark.css" />`, then a `<style>` element LAST containing `:root {` and `--accent: #c14bd0;`. THE FINGERPRINT TRAP IS ABSENT: the themes/ href is exactly `themes/huddle-dark.css` with no hash, while the other three are fingerprinted - so the theme link is hand-built and did not go through the asset helper that returns an unresolved key verbatim. EVERY stylesheet round-trips 200: themes/huddle-dark.css, themes/huddle-light.css, theme.*.css, app.*.css and Huddle.App.*.styles.css - zero 404s. Both theme files return the documented five-line comment plus a :root block whose only declaration is `color-scheme: dark;` / `color-scheme: light;` respectively. BOTH NEGATIVE CASES HOLD: with the theme set but no overrides there are ZERO <style> elements and still one themes/ link; with `System` selected (empty theme) there are ZERO <style> elements AND ZERO themes/ links, so a Theme never pins color-scheme when System is chosen. No unhandled-error banner. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-06](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-06-the-saved-theme-is-shown-as-selected-in-the-dropdown-after-a-full-load) |  | Pass |  | All three states checked against the RAW server HTML, so the `selected` attribute read is the PRERENDERED value - which also means the dropdown never transiently shows `System` before settling, since the correct option arrives already marked selected in the first byte of HTML. `{ "theme": "huddle-dark" }` -> head carries themes/huddle-dark.css and the selected option value is `huddle-dark` (Dark), page dark. `{ "theme": "huddle-light" }` -> head carries themes/huddle-light.css and the selected option is `huddle-light` (Light). No file at all -> ZERO themes/ links and the selected option value is the empty string (System). File value, dropdown selection and the <head> link agree in every one of the three cases. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-07](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-07-no-flash-of-light-on-a-hard-reload-with-dark-chosen) |  | Pass |  | Judged structurally rather than by eye, because this harness cannot capture a first-paint filmstrip - and the structural oracle is exactly what the Fail-if names. Both mechanisms that would cause a flash are ruled out. (1) THE LINK IS EMITTED AND EARLY: with `{ "theme": "huddle-dark" }`, the raw server HTML for /, /teammates and /settings/appearance all carry `themes/huddle-dark.css` inside <head>, and on every one of the three routes NO <script> tag precedes it - so it is a render-blocking stylesheet applied before first paint by definition. (2) IT IS NOT 404ing: APPEARANCETHEME-05 confirmed themes/huddle-dark.css returns 200. (3) NO CLIENT-SIDE LOADER EXISTS: grepping every .js under wwwroot for `localStorage`, `prefers-color-scheme`, `color-scheme` or `theme` returned ZERO hits, so nothing applies the theme after paint - the signature of the flash this test hunts. Recorded honestly: the frame-by-frame visual confirmation and the Slow-4G throttled reload were not performed. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-08](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-08-system-follows-the-operating-system-live-with-no-reload-an-explicit-theme-ignores-the-os-entirely) |  | Pass |  | METHOD NOTE, important: the Claude browser pane forces a prefers-color-scheme emulation to match the Claude app theme, so DevTools `No emulation` is unreachable here (the pane was reporting dark while the OS is genuinely Light). The OS flip was therefore substituted by flipping the pane's own prefers-color-scheme emulation, which delivers the identical media-query signal the OS sends. What that proves is the APP's response; it does not re-prove that Windows drives the query, which is Chrome's job. SYSTEM HALF: with no override file (zero themes/ links confirmed in the raw HTML), flipping the signal light->dark repainted the application IMMEDIATELY with NO reload and NO navigation - the JS marker window.__m survived and navigation entries stayed at 1. The whole page moved together: body background rgb(255,255,255)->rgb(27,27,31), body colour rgb(34,34,34)->rgb(230,230,234), sidebar rgb(245,245,245)->rgb(35,35,40), and the native <select> control rgb(255,255,255)->rgb(59,59,59). The two required computed values match exactly: rgb(255, 255, 255) in light and rgb(27, 27, 31) in dark. EXPLICIT THEME HALF: with `huddle-dark` chosen, the app is dark on a light signal (rgb(27,27,31)) and stayed rgb(27,27,31) through a flip to dark and back - it ignores the OS entirely. Mirror case: with `huddle-light` chosen, the app is light on a DARK signal (rgb(255,255,255)) and stayed light after flipping back. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-09](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-09-the-theme-choice-is-per-installation-not-per-browser) |  | Pass |  | The strongest available evidence that the choice is per INSTALLATION: a completely stateless client - Invoke-WebRequest, which has no cookies, no localStorage and no profile at all - fetched / and got `themes/huddle-dark.css` in <head>. Browser storage is empty in every sense: localStorage has 0 keys, sessionStorage 0 keys, document.cookie is the empty string - nothing about themes, appearance or colours anywhere. A brand-new browser tab (fresh circuit, no prior state) rendered dark immediately with the themes/huddle-dark.css link and body rgb(27,27,31), with no setup. Clearing localStorage + sessionStorage + all cookies and reloading left the app DARK (rgb(27,27,31)) with storage still empty. Stopping and restarting the application left it dark - the stateless fetch after restart still carried the themes link. SUBSTITUTION NOTE: a second physically different browser (Edge/Firefox) and a real Incognito window were not available in this harness; the stateless HTTP client plus the empty-storage evidence cover the same claim more directly, since there is no client-side store for a per-browser choice to live in. |

The raw server <style> element reads literally `:root {` / `    --accent: #c14bd0;` / `    --font-chat: Georgia, serif;` / `}` - declarations SORTED BY TOKEN NAME (accent before font-chat), and containing NO HTML entities (checked for &quot; &lt; &gt; &amp; - none present), so the CSS is not being escaped. The Appearance tab shows NO `Overrides that didn't load` section. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-11](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-11-exotic-but-allowed-override-values-survive-including-a-quoted-font-name) |  | Pass |  | All four exotic values ACCEPTED, none listed under `Overrides that didn't load`, and none HTML-escaped. (1) `"Segoe UI", sans-serif` -> the <style> contains the raw characters `--font-ui: "Segoe UI", sans-serif;` with REAL double-quote characters, not &quot;. (2) `125%` -> emitted as `--font-size-base: 125%;`. (3) `color-mix(in srgb, #ffffff 50%, #000000)` -> emitted verbatim including the parentheses and percentage. (4) `var(--font-mono)` -> emitted verbatim AND it resolved: the Room's message text computes fontFamily `ui-monospace, "Cascadia Mono", Consolas, "Courier New", monospace`, proving a value may reference another token and that the declaration wins the cascade rather than merely reaching <head>. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-12](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-12-a-hostile-or-malformed-override-value-is-rejected-reported-on-the-tab-logged-once-and-the-file-is-left-byte-for-byte-unchanged) |  | Pass |  | All SEVEN hostile values handled correctly, with the security property intact in every case. THE CATASTROPHIC CASE DID NOT OCCUR: the substring `} :root{` never appears in the served document for any of the seven, and NO <style> element is emitted in <head> when nothing valid survives. The application renders normally in dark throughout (body rgb(27,27,31), layout intact, no stray CSS text, #blazor-error-ui display:none). The log carries EXACTLY SEVEN `rejected an override` warnings - one per case - with the specified wording: `red; } :root{` and `#fff /* hi */` and `<script>` and `#fff\a` all give `contains a character that is not allowed`; `url(banner.png)` gives `contains a disallowed sequence`; the blank value and the 201-character value both give `value must be 1-200 characters once trimmed`. Each ends `it was left in the file and ignored.` The rejection is also SURFACED ON THE TAB, not just logged - the Appearance panel renders an `Overrides that didn't load` section listing the entry. THE FILE IS NEVER TOUCHED: contents byte-identical (ordinal comparison) and LastWriteTimeUtc unchanged after every one of the seven loads, so the app never rewrites or 'fixes' the Human's text. |

THE KEY STRUCTURAL FACT: `color-scheme` computes to `dark` on :root, which is what makes the browser paint dark scrollbars and dark native <select> popups. THE ONLY TWO LIGHT ELEMENTS IN THE WHOLE DOM ARE BOTH HIDDEN AND BOTH EXPECTED: #blazor-error-ui at lightyellow rgb(255,255,224) - documented exemption (1), deliberately a literal so it stays readable when the app has already failed - and #components-reconnect-modal at white; both compute display:none with zero client rects, so neither APPEARS. Documented exemption (2) also confirmed present rather than treated as a defect: the card's Persona markdown keeps the inherited sans-serif stack rather than the monospace token. NOT OBSERVED, recorded honestly: the genuinely visual sub-checks this harness cannot make - the native <select> POPUP menu being dark, and scrollbar rendering - are covered only indirectly, by color-scheme:dark reaching :root. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-23](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-23-hover-selection-and-focus-surfaces-all-switch-and-the-four-old-hover-colours-are-now-one) |  | Fail | [#25](http://gitea-host.example:3000/emre/Huddle/issues/25) | TWO OF THREE PASS CONDITIONS HOLD; THE FOCUS ONE DOES NOT. (1) HOVER IS ONE TOKEN: enumerating every :hover rule that sets a background across all loaded stylesheets, EVERY app-authored one reads a token, never a literal - .room-list li a:hover, .invite-agent:hover and .teammate-tile:hover (the three this test names) all read the SAME `var(--surface-hover)`, as do .new-chat-toggle, .sidebar-link, .invite-toggle, .draft-stop, .teammate-card-action and .settings-tab. That token resolves to rgb(50,50,59) in dark - a dark value, and none of the four pale literals the Fail-if lists. The only non-token hover backgrounds are a transparent rgba(0,0,0,0) in third-party MudBlazor.min.css and a ::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb:hover grey; neither is a row highlight. (2) SELECTED IS DISTINCT AND DARK: --surface-selected resolves to rgb(47,61,87) and the active room row computes exactly that - dark, and different from the hover value. (3) FOCUS OUTLINE IS NOT VISIBLE - THIS IS THE FAILURE. After a REAL keyboard Tab, the focused element matches :focus-visible yet computes `outline-style: none`, so no ring is drawn: anchors and buttons both. Cause located precisely: `a:focus-visible { outline: none }` in the THIRD-PARTY MudBlazor.min.css; the app's own app.css and theme.css contain zero `outline` and zero `:focus` rules. The composer textarea is the exception and does get a visible ring (outline-style `auto`, rgb(238,238,238) against the rgb(27,27,31) ground). THE CAUSE IS NOT THE ONE THE FAIL-IF PREDICTS: it is not a colour that fails to switch - the outline COLOUR resolves to a high-contrast light value in dark. It is the outline being suppressed outright, and it is THEME-INDEPENDENT: re-checked under huddle-light on a white ground, links and buttons still compute outline-style `none`. So this is an accessibility defect in the shell rather than a tokenisation regression, which is why it is filed separately rather than against the theme layer. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-24](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-24-the-reconnect-modal-shows-one-themed-state-paragraph-over-a-dimmed-backdrop-in-both-themes) |  | Fail | [#26](http://gitea-host.example:3000/emre/Huddle/issues/26), [#16](http://gitea-host.example:3000/emre/Huddle/issues/16) | TWO OF THE PASS CONDITIONS HOLD, TWO DO NOT. HOLDS (1) THE ASSET-KEY TRAP IS ABSENT: the scoped bundle Huddle.App.j8c8gsv1bg.styles.css is linked and all five CSS requests return 200, so the display:none/display:block state machine is loaded and the six state paragraphs are NOT all showing at once. HOLDS (2) THE BACKDROP IS THEMED AND DIMMED: ::backdrop reads var(--scrim) and resolves to the exact values the test names - rgba(0,0,0,0.6) under huddle-dark and rgba(0,0,0,0.4) under huddle-light - so the dimming really is per-theme, not a hardcoded scrim. FAILS (1) EXACTLY ONE STATE PARAGRAPH: at first drop the modal is `components-reconnect-show` with ONE visible paragraph `Rejoining the server...` plus the animation - correct - but after ~9s it becomes `components-reconnect-show components-reconnect-retrying` and TWO paragraphs are visible simultaneously, `Rejoining the server...` AND `Rejoin failed... trying again in N seconds.`, because the retrying class is additive and never clears the show-state rule. Reproduced in BOTH themes (dark at 4s remaining, light at 30s remaining). This is the already-filed #16 / SHELLNAV-26 defect, linked rather than duplicated. FAILS (2) THE PANEL IS A THEMED RAISED SURFACE - NEW, filed as #26: under huddle-dark the dialog computes background-color rgb(255,255,255) while its text computes rgb(230,230,234), i.e. near-white on white, about 1.06:1 contrast - the state text is unreadable precisely when it matters. CAUSE LOCATED EXACTLY: the app's scoped rule #components-reconnect-modal[b-3dgaftw3nf] sets background-color: var(--surface-raised), but _content/MudBlazor/MudBlazor.min.css ships `#components-reconnect-modal{z-index:9999 !important;background-color:var(--mud-palette-background) !important}` and !important outranks the higher specificity; --mud-palette-background computes rgba(255,255,255,1) because MudBlazor is never switched to its dark palette. It is NOT a token or light-dark() failure: `color: var(--text-primary)` from the SAME rule block wins and resolves to the dark branch, border-radius 8px and the box-shadow from that block apply, and a probe div with color-scheme:dark resolves light-dark(#ffffff,#2a2a31) to rgb(42,42,49) correctly. The light theme MASKS this completely, since --surface-raised's light branch is #ffffff anyway. Retry button rgb(94,43,96) on rgb(255,255,255) survives because MudBlazor restyles the button separately. |
| [APPEARANCETHEME-25](appearance-theme.md#appearancetheme-25-the-layering-probe-a-theme-that-sets-one-token-inherits-the-whole-built-in-dark-palette-for-the-rest) |  | Pass |  | THE FALL-THROUGH THE WHOLE DESIGN RESTS ON IS REAL. Probe theme created with ONLY `color-scheme: dark` and `--accent: #ff00ff` inside :root, plus the `probe` ThemeDescriptor after huddle-dark; `dotnet build Huddle.slnx` reported 0 Warning(s) 0 Error(s). The dropdown then offered a FOURTH option `Probe`, and selecting it wrote theme=probe and loaded themes/probe.css as the only theme link. WITH color-scheme:dark PRESENT, against a LIGHT operating system (prefers-color-scheme:dark = false), :root computed color-scheme `dark` and every unmapped token resolved to the BUILT-IN DARK value: body rgb(27,27,31) exactly as specified, sidebar rgb(35,35,40), teammate tile rgb(42,42,49), text rgb(230,230,234) - while the one mapped token showed through as the magenta, `New teammate` button rgb(255,0,255). NOTHING WAS BLANK OR TRANSPARENT: a programmatic sweep of every element with a client rect found exactly four surfaces averaging >140 per channel and all four ARE the accent itself (the button and three teammate avatars, all rgb(255,0,255)); no element rendered with no colour. THE SECOND HALF REPRODUCES THE DOCUMENTED TRAP RATHER THAN A BUG: with `color-scheme: dark;` REMOVED and the OS light, :root falls back to the base stylesheet's `light dark`, so the unmapped tokens follow the OS and go LIGHT - body rgb(255,255,255), sidebar rgb(245,245,245), tile rgb(255,255,255), text rgb(34,34,34) - while --accent stays #ff00ff and the button stays rgb(255,0,255). Confirmed the served probe.css genuinely no longer contained the declaration before reading this. CLEAN-UP VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED: probe.css deleted (GET /themes/probe.css now 404), the ThemeDescriptor line removed and the file restored byte-identical to HEAD (git status shows no source modification), `dotnet build Huddle.slnx` green again at 0/0, the dropdown back to exactly System, Light, Dark, and --accent back to the built-in light-dark(#4a154b, #5e2b60). SUBSTITUTION NOTE: the OS light/dark switch was made through the browser pane's prefers-color-scheme emulation rather than the Windows setting, because the Claude browser pane forces that media query to follow the app theme and DevTools 'No emulation' is unreachable here - the same substitution recorded on APPEARANCETHEME-08. The Windows setting itself is genuinely Light (AppsUseLightTheme=1), so the emulated value matches the real one. |

| [PIPEEXTERNAL-01](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-01-the-two-demo-agents-are-already-there-and-they-reply-to-a-mention) |  | Pass |  | Lane P-LAUNCH-FREE (--Team:Acp:Enabled=false), no Team__DemoAgent__* variable set (Get-ChildItem Env:Team__DemoAgent__* prints nothing). The sidebar Room list contains an entry reading exactly `echo` and one reading exactly `alpha`, each ONCE - counted programmatically over .room-list li a, not eyeballed; 16 other Rooms from earlier areas are present, which the test allows. The app log carries EXACTLY ONE `Demo agent echo connected.` and EXACTLY ONE `Demo agent alpha connected.` (counted with Select-String, so the doubling failure - a pre-populated DemoAgentOptions.Names default the binder appends to - is ruled out rather than assumed). Opening `echo`: h1 reads `echo` and the line directly beneath reads `You, echo`; composer placeholder is `Message… (/invite @agent)`. Sending `hi @echo` produced a new row whose sender is `echo` and whose body HTML is `<p><strong>echo:</strong> hi echo</p>` - genuinely BOLD markup, not the literal text `**echo:**`, and with NO `@` left in it (so the demo client is still stripping @ from quoted text and the amplification bug is absent). Both the human row and the agent row carry a sender name and an HH:mm timestamp (`You 23:49`, `echo 23:49`). The `alpha` Room behaved identically: h1 `alpha`, reply sender `alpha`, body `<p><strong>alpha:</strong> hi alpha</p>`. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-02](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-02-a-demo-agent-streams-a-draft-then-replaces-it-with-the-finished-message) |  | Pass |  | The ~120ms stream was captured with a MutationObserver on .message-list rather than by eye, which the test's Inconclusive clause explicitly allows as the fallback oracle - and it gave MORE than the DOM-node check it asks for. THE TRANSIENT ROW: class is exactly `message-row streaming`; it carries <span class="message-sender">echo</span> and a <button type="button" class="draft-stop">Stop</button> beside the name; its body is <div class="message-body streaming"> ending in <span class="draft-caret"></span>. THE CARET BLINKS: .draft-caret has `animation: draft-caret-blink 1s step-end infinite` with a keyframe flipping border-color to transparent at 50%. THREE VISIBLE CHUNKS, captured verbatim as the text grew: `**echo` then `**echo:** hi` then `**echo:** hi echo` - so the body is PLAIN while streaming, the `**echo:**` prefix showing as literal asterisks, never pushed through the markdown renderer. THE REPLACEMENT IS CLEAN: the streaming row is removed and exactly one finished row remains, rendered bold as <p><strong>echo:</strong> hi echo</p>. NEVER BOTH AT ONCE - MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: a timestamped run showed the finished row inserted at t=64194ms and the streaming row gone at t=64196ms, an overlap of 2 MILLISECONDS, about an eighth of one 16.7ms frame, so the reply cannot show twice on screen; and it certainly does not `remain`. (The stronger requestAnimationFrame oracle - a sample taken immediately before paint - could not be used: rAF never fires in this browser pane because the tab is not painting, 0 frames over 3 seconds. The 2ms mutation window is reported instead of guessing.) TRANSCRIPT: App_Data/rooms/01a0a3ae26dd774f934b0e01fae58bdf.jsonl ends with the human Message then the `echo` Message, in that order, and a case-insensitive grep for `draft` over the whole file returns ZERO lines, so no Draft is persisted. |

The app logged EXACTLY `Created room '01a0a3d4a1847ce39002b1e0eb2b67e7' (echo, alpha) with 3 members.` MENTION GATING HOLDS BOTH WAYS: `hi @echo` produced exactly ONE new Message, sender `echo`, body <p><strong>echo:</strong> hi echo</p> - the Room went from 0 rows to 2 (the human's and echo's), so `alpha` posted nothing even though it is a Member and received the envelope. The bare `hi` with no @ produced NOTHING over a full 11 seconds: the Room stayed at 3 rows, the third being the human's own `hi`. NOTE ON THE team.db CHECK (a step, not a pass condition): team.db exists but its own LastWriteTime is ~43 minutes old, which is NOT a miss - the database is in WAL mode, so the seconds-old write lands in team.db-wal (16 seconds old, 865 KB) and team.db-shm (194 seconds old). The welcome line was parsed rather than eyeballed. STARTS `{"type":"welcome",` - true by StartsWith. ENDS `,"version":3}` - true by EndsWith, so the strict version is 3, NOT the stale 2 that docs/AgencyTeam.md's sample shows (the NOTE's warning; the doc is wrong, the app is right). agentId = 01a0a3d524087d4b90b5cb4aae7b5296, LENGTH EXACTLY 32 and matching ^[0-9a-f]{32}$ - so lowercase hex, no dashes, no uppercase. name = `mybot2`, exactly the -Name argument. rooms holds EXACTLY ONE entry, name `mybot2`, id 01a0a3d5240e7a92821c1af407642ae0 - WHICH IS THE ID IN THE BROWSER ADDRESS BAR with that Room open (/rooms/01a0a3d5240e7a92821c1af407642ae0), so the two sides agree rather than merely looking similar. Its members array holds EXACTLY TWO objects: {id:human, name:You, kind:human} and {id:01a0a3d524087d4b90b5cb4aae7b5296, name:mybot2, kind:agent} - the agent entry's id is the same agentId, and the human entry is present, so a client reading the member count for its reply gate sees 2. NO PROPERTY IS MISSPELLED: the top level is exactly type,agentId,name,rooms,version (so the `type` discriminator IS there and it is not agent_id); a room is exactly id,name,members (not roomName); a member is exactly id,name,kind. |

Logging config is not the cause - appsettings.Development.json sets Agency.Huddle to Debug. BUT THE TEST'S STATED CONSEQUENCE IS DISPROVEN, which is why this is filed narrowly as an observability gap rather than as the teardown defect: the Fail-if says 'the connection teardown path is not running, and the Agent may still be registered as online' - it IS running, because immediately after the kill the New chat panel reported mybot2 as `agent-dot offline` / title `offline` while echo and alpha stayed online, so gateway.Unregister(this) in the finally executed. Cause located at src/Huddle.App/Pipes/AgentConnection.cs:106 - the Warning sits in `catch (IOException)` only, and a client closing its end cleanly (Ctrl+C or a killed process) ends ReadLoopAsync at end of stream, so RunAsync returns normally and no catch is entered; OperationCanceledException and ObjectDisposedException are deliberately silent. Filed as #27 with the suggestion to log one Information line in the finally beside the Unregister. |

| [PIPEEXTERNAL-14](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-14-an-invalid-name-is-refused-with-invalidname-the-connection-closes-and-no-room-appears) |  | Pass |  | ALL SIX shapes refused with `invalidName`, each naming the exact Name passed, then `Server closed the connection.`, and the script exited every time: `bad name!`, `my  bot` (doubled space), ` mybot` (leading space), `mybot ` (trailing space), `-bot` (leading hyphen) and `bot.1` (dot). Envelope form, e.g. {"type":"error","code":"invalidName","message":"\u0027bad name!\u0027 is not a valid agent name.","version":3} - the apostrophes arrive as \u0027 rather than raw, which is the same wire-JSON escaping the area's own NOTE says is correct and not a fail. THE CODE IS `invalidName`, NOT `badMessage`, in all six - so a client can tell a bad Name from a bad envelope. THE CONNECTION IS ACTUALLY CLOSED, not merely refused: the script's own Dispose then throws `Cannot access a closed pipe.`, so no rejected client is left holding a pipe instance. NOTHING WAS CREATED: the sidebar held 21 Rooms before the six attempts and 21 after, INCLUDING after a hard F5 refresh, and none of the six spellings appears as a Room; the New chat panel lists exactly echo, alpha, mybot, gamma, Emily Lee, delta, coo, Jarvis, Friday, mybot2, ordercheck, so team.db gained no row for any of them either. (A pre-existing `mybot` Room from an earlier area is NOT one of these - it does not match any of the six spellings exactly.) HARNESS NOTE: the first attempt at this test hit exactly the documented Inconclusive trap - Start-Process -ArgumentList passes its string through the Windows command-line parser, which understands only DOUBLE quotes, so `-Name 'bad name!'` split on the space and the client dialled pipe `\\.\pipe\name!'` as agent `'bad`, timing out without ever reaching the server. That run was discarded and every name re-run through a path that quotes correctly; the results above are from the clean runs. |

THE SECOND IS ACCEPTED, NOT REFUSED: it received a normal `welcome` carrying the SAME agentId 01a0a3e30576746b8aaf2ccd7c61602d - so a client that crashed without a clean disconnect can get back in without an app restart. THE DISPLACED CLIENT IS TOLD, not left hanging - this is the half the test calls the silent failure: its console's last line is `Server closed the connection.` (its own Dispose then reports `Cannot access a closed pipe.`) and the PROCESS EXITED, confirmed by Get-Process returning nothing for its pid while the second process is still alive. THE BROWSER SHOWED NO CHANGE AT ALL: a snapshot of the full sidebar (27 entries), the heading `dup1`, the members line `You, dup1` and every transcript row was taken before the second connect and compared by exact string equality afterwards - IDENTICAL, so displacement is not being treated as a Room-level event. NO ZOMBIE: `hi @dup1 after displacement` was delivered to the SECOND console only (its messagePosted count went to 1 with that text; the first console stayed at the 1 line it had from before displacement), exactly ONE new agent Message appeared, and the Room's .jsonl went from 2 lines to 4 - exactly two added, never three. |

Each round launched three clients back to back in a tight loop with no wait between them, which is tighter than pressing Enter by hand in three terminals. ROUND b1/b2/b3, ROUND c1/c2/c3, ROUND d1/d2/d3: in every round all three printed a {"type":"welcome" line - NINE welcomes, zero failures. NO ROUND PRODUCED `All pipe instances are busy`: every client's stderr was read, not just its stdout, and none carried that exception or any other - so the next pipe server instance is being created before I/O starts on the accepted one. THE IDS ARE DISTINCT WITHIN EVERY ROUND: 3 unique of 3 each time (b: ...4bc2e97f / ...aa49ff93 / ...2c4a83a3; c: ...08677efc / ...79563bda / ...d2b48d8e; d: ...5a66ae77 / ...b60eaa3d / ...35939f46), so distinct Names are not collapsing onto one Agent. THE APP LOGGED EXACTLY THREE `Created direct room` lines PER ROUND, counted as a delta around each round - so no connection was accepted and then left without completing its handshake. ALL NINE Rooms are in the sidebar (b1,b2,b3,c1,c2,c3,d1,d2,d3 all present, none missing) and they arrived with performance navigation entries still 1, i.e. with no refresh. The app stayed up throughout, so no round is disqualified as a timeout. |

Room 01a0a3e4ebb37743b2e122edeac3e21c, heading `bud1, bud2`, members `You, bud1, bud2`. THE LABELS RIDE ON THE WIRE: the line delivered to BOTH bots for the human's Message ends `"agentMessagesSinceHuman":0,"budget":1` - used 0, granted 1, so a client can apply the rule itself. THE GUARD IS ENFORCED, NOT JUST ADVERTISED: exactly ONE reply landed (bud2's, this run - which of the two wins is documented as non-deterministic and either is a pass) and it is the only agent row in the browser. THE OTHER BOT GOT THE SPECIFIC, TERMINAL CODE: bud1's console printed {"type":"error","code":"budgetExhausted","message":"This room has reached its budget of 1 agent messages since a human last spoke. Do not retry: further posts to this room will be refused until a human speaks here.","relatedMessageId":"3b420b7d06db48d280536ebf4c2fe069","version":3} - `budgetExhausted`, NOT a generic badMessage, and it carries the do-not-retry instruction and the id of the post it refused. THE COUNTER MOVED: the line delivered to bud1 for bud2's reply ends `"agentMessagesSinceHuman":1,"budget":1`. The app logged the Warning EXACTLY as specified: `Room '01a0a3e4ebb37743b2e122edeac3e21c' refused a message from 'bud1': its budget of 1 agent messages since a human last spoke is spent.` THE HUMAN IS ASKED, AND IN THE RIGHT PLACE: walking the DOM from the message list to the composer finds exactly one element between them, div.budget-prompt, reading `Agents have sent 1 replies since you last spoke, and are paused.` with two enabled buttons, `Continue` and `Leave paused` - below the transcript, not above it. THE REFUSAL HAPPENED BEFORE PERSISTENCE: the Room's .jsonl holds only the human line and bud2's reply; bud1's refused text is absent. |

Before Continue: 2 transcript rows, last row `bud2: **bud2:** hi bud1 bud2`; the refused bot bud1's last delivered messagePosted had message.id 44db3ad2cd264c30b3f90c6b27d69729, used=1, budget=1. AFTER CLICKING CONTINUE, bud1's console printed a THIRD messagePosted line for a Message IT HAD ALREADY SEEN - same message.id 44db3ad2cd264c30b3f90c6b27d69729 and same text `**bud2:** hi bud1 bud2` - and that line now ends `"budget":2`, so the granted figure went up on the wire. THE RE-DELIVERY IS INVISIBLE: the browser row count is UNCHANGED at 2, with the same two rows in the same order - nothing rendered twice. The app logged `Room '01a0a3e4ebb37743b2e122edeac3e21c' was extended to 2 agent messages.` The pause block was replaced by div.budget-note reading EXACTLY `1 of 2 agent replies since you last spoke.` NO NEW REPLY FOLLOWED, which the Inconclusive clause says is CORRECT rather than a fail - the re-delivered Message is bud2's reply, whose `@` was stripped, so a mention-gated client rightly stays silent; the evidence here is the re-delivered line plus the unchanged transcript, and bud1's console was definitely not silent. THE DOUBLE-CLICK GUARD HOLDS: on a fresh pause the button was clicked once and then eight more times at 5ms intervals; a MutationObserver recorded the state sequence `Continue\|false` -> `Continuing…\|true` -> (prompt gone), so the button both RELABELS to `Continuing…` and is DISABLED while the grant is in flight. The app log holds exactly TWO `was extended to` lines in total - one from the earlier single click and one from this whole double-click burst - so nine clicks granted once. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-28](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-28-a-wrong-protocol-version-in-hello-is-refused-and-the-browser-never-learns) |  | Pass |  | Done with the RAW-CLIENT rather than by editing tools/echo-bot.ps1, which the NOTE forbids. A version-2 hello returned EXACTLY {"type":"error","code":"expectedHello","message":"Unsupported protocol version \u00272\u0027.","version":3} - the \u0027 are the ordinary wire-JSON escaping of the quotes around the 2. The NEXT ReadLine() returned $null, so the server closed the connection rather than leaving a mismatched client hanging. STRICT EQUALITY IS INTACT: no `vtest` Room was created. THE BROWSER LEARNED NOTHING, before or after an F5: the Room list stayed at 40 entries with no `vtest`, and a scan of the page text found zero occurrences of error/unsupported/version - no Room, no error, no visible trace, exactly as documented. THE TWO SIDES AGREE ON THE NUMBER: src/Huddle.Contracts/ProtocolVersion.cs reads `public const int Current = 3;` (its doc comment even says 'A strict-equality check against every parsed envelope') and tools/echo-bot.ps1 sends `version = 3` in both places it appears - so the shipped client was not left behind by a version bump. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-29](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-29-a-first-message-that-is-not-hello-and-a-silent-client-are-both-refused-as-expectedhello) |  | Pass |  | AN UNAUTHENTICATED CLIENT CANNOT POST. Sending {"type":"postMessage",...,"roomId":"x","text":"hi"} as the FIRST message returned {"type":"error","code":"expectedHello","message":"Expected \u0027hello\u0027 as the first message.","version":3} - the specific code, not a generic one - and the next ReadLine() returned $null, so the connection was closed rather than allowed to continue. THE SILENT CLIENT IS TIMED OUT, NOT LEFT HOLDING A PIPE: a second raw connection that sent nothing had its first ReadLine() return after 5.00 SECONDS - measured with a Stopwatch - carrying {"type":"error","code":"expectedHello","message":"Timed out waiting for \u0027hello\u0027.","version":3}. So the hello timeout is alive and a stuck or hostile client cannot hold an instance indefinitely. It did NOT return instantly with nothing, which the Inconclusive clause says would have been a distinct finding. The following ReadLine() returned $null there too. THE BROWSER GAINED NOTHING from either attempt, before or after an F5: 40 Rooms both times, no Room named `x`, and since no such Room exists no transcript could have grown. |

(a) bad JSON -> "code":"badMessage" with the JSON library's own complaint, `'not json at all' is an invalid JSON literal. Expected the literal 'null'. Path: $ \| LineNumber: 0 \| BytePositionInLine: 1.` (not pinned, as instructed). (b) second hello -> "code":"badMessage", "message":"already registered". (c) unknown room -> "code":"unknownRoom", "message":"Unknown room 'nosuchroom'.", "relatedMessageId":"m9". (d) empty text -> "code":"badMessage", "message":"empty message", "relatedMessageId":"m10". (e) bad message id -> "code":"badMessage", "message":"Invalid message id.", "relatedMessageId":"m.1". Every one uses the SPECIFIC documented code, and every refusal of a POST carries relatedMessageId so the client knows which in-flight post failed. (a) and (b) carry none, which is correct rather than the omission the Fail-if describes: unparseable text has no id to quote and a hello is not a post.) THE CONNECTION SURVIVED ALL FIVE: the sixth request, a valid post with messageId m11, SUCCEEDED - `still alive` appears in the raw1 Room in the browser with NO refresh (performance navigation entries still 1) and the Room opened straight from the sidebar entry that had appeared live. VALIDATION IS BEFORE PERSISTENCE: the Room's .jsonl holds EXACTLY ONE line, the m11 `still alive` Message; none of the five refused requests reached it. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-31](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-31-an-agent-cannot-write-a-draft-into-a-room-it-is-not-a-member-of-even-by-reusing-a-message-id) |  | Pass |  | ECHOROOM = 01a0a3ae26dd774f934b0e01fae58bdf (8 existing rows). THE INTRUSION IS REFUSED WITH THE RIGHT DIAGNOSIS: {"type":"error","code":"notMember","message":"'01a0a3e7ee5273ceb84e8cbac7954361' is not a member of room '01a0a3ae26dd774f934b0e01fae58bdf'.","relatedMessageId":"m1","version":3} - `notMember`, NOT `unknownRoom`, so the room lookup is not failing ahead of the membership check. THE LEGITIMATE DRAFT IS ACCEPTED: the same messageId `m1` written into raw2's OWN Room produced no error line and a real streaming row - a second browser tab watching that Room recorded 67 samples carrying `message-row streaming` reading `raw2\|Stop\|mine`. THE LAUNDERING ATTEMPT STILL FAILS: retrying the intrusion with that same id `m1`, immediately after it had been accepted elsewhere, returned the IDENTICAL `notMember` error with relatedMessageId `m1` - so the verdict is keyed on the Room, not cached on the message id. THE ECHO ROOM NEVER SHOWED ANYTHING: a poller on that Room across the whole exchange recorded a constant 8 rows, ZERO streaming rows and ZERO samples where the page text contained `intruder`, and a direct check afterwards confirms `intruder`/`intruder again` are absent. (Honest note on resolution: the echo tab was backgrounded for part of the run so its poller was throttled to roughly 1 sample/second, 43 samples; the row count and the text check are unambiguous at that rate, and a refused delta is never stored so there was nothing that could have rendered between samples.) |

THE ONE FAILING CONDITION is `T-A logs the connection ending (Agent connection <id> ended.)`: the app logged only `Created direct room '01a0a3e8e2c4778fb323ee5d448ebfd6' for agent 'rawdraft'.` and nothing at all for the disconnect. This is NOT a second defect - it is the same already-filed #27 (the Warning sits only in catch (IOException) at AgentConnection.cs:106, and a clean close never throws), reached here through a different client. Recorded as a Fail because the condition is unmet, with the link rather than a new issue. |

THE EXTERNAL BOT TOOK OVER THE IDENTITY rather than creating a second Agent: connecting `pwsh tools/echo-bot.ps1 -Name echo` returned agentId 01a0a3ae26d670d5af2f636c71464078 - THE SAME id the demo agent had, verified against the earlier `Invited agent 'echo' (01a0a3ae26d670d5af2f636c71464078)` log line from PIPEEXTERNAL-18 - and its welcome listed 12 existing Rooms including the existing echo Room 01a0a3ae26dd774f934b0e01fae58bdf. NO NEW `echo` ROOM APPEARED. It was NOT refused, so displacement semantics are unchanged and consistent with PIPEEXTERNAL-24. AFTER KILLING THE EXTERNAL BOT, `echo` IS DEAD FOR THE LIFE OF THE PROCESS: `hi @echo after trap` got no reply over 31 seconds (12 rows -> 13, the human's own), and a second attempt `hi @echo second try` got none over 16 more (13 -> 14). The demo agent did NOT reconnect by itself. THE UI SAYS NOTHING: zero role="alert" elements, no banner/badge/alert-classed element visible anywhere, and nothing at all between the transcript and the message box - the Room looks entirely normal. The New chat panel does show the truth in the one place that reads presence: `echo` is `agent-dot offline` / title `offline` / rgb(224,90,90), while `alpha` is still `agent-dot online` / rgb(63,191,90). THE ABSENCE OF A LOG LINE IS THE FINDING AND IT IS CONFIRMED: scanning the ENTIRE app log for any line containing `echo` or `Demo agent` returns exactly TWO lines, both from startup (`Demo agent echo connected.`, `Demo agent alpha connected.`) - the whole log is 14 lines. There is no `Demo agent echo stopped unexpectedly.`, no warning, nothing. AFTER THE APP RESTART everything recovers: the log again reads `Demo agent echo connected.` and `hi @echo after restart` is answered with `echo: **echo:** hi echo after restart` - so nothing was persisted that should not have been. Run last among the free tests and followed by the required restart, as the test instructs. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-34](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-34-stopping-the-app-closes-every-agent-connection-cleanly-and-the-browsers-reconnect-dialog-is-readable) |  | Fail | [#16](http://gitea-host.example:3000/emre/Huddle/issues/16) | FOUR OF THE FIVE PASS CONDITIONS HOLD. SHUTDOWN TELLS EVERY CLIENT: both shut1 and shut2 printed `Server closed the connection.` as their last line and BOTH PROCESSES EXITED - a scan for pwsh processes running echo-bot afterwards returns zero - so no agent has to be killed by hand. THE DIALOG SEQUENCE IS RIGHT: sampled every 200ms across the whole shutdown, it goes `components-reconnect-show` -> `components-reconnect-show components-reconnect-retrying` (with the 5s then 30s backoff counting down) -> `components-reconnect-failed` showing `Failed to rejoin.Please retry or reload the page.` with a Retry button, about 81 seconds in. STATE SURVIVED THE RESTART: after Retry, the same Room URL, the sidebar still holds 45 Rooms and the transcript still holds its 10 rows ending `echo: **echo:** hi echo before shutdown` - nothing lost. THE COUNTERS RESET: the div.budget-note reading `1 of 40 agent replies since you last spoke.` that was showing before the shutdown is GONE afterwards, so the budget is in-memory as documented. THE FAILING CONDITION is `At each stage the dialog shows exactly ONE state paragraph`: the FIRST stage shows one and the FAILED stage shows one, but the RETRYING stage shows TWO stacked - `Rejoining the server...` AND `Rejoin failed... trying again in N seconds.` - and it stays that way for the entire ~44-second retry window, not for an instant. THE CAUSE IS NOT THE ONE THIS TEST PREDICTS: its Fail-if blames a 404 on the ReconnectModal CSS, but fetching every linked stylesheet returns 200 for ALL FIVE including the scoped bundle Huddle.App.j8c8gsv1bg.styles.css, and the dialog picks up border-radius and box-shadow from that same bundle. The real cause is that Blazor ADDS `components-reconnect-retrying` alongside `components-reconnect-show` rather than replacing it, so the first-attempt rule keeps matching. Linked to the existing #16 (also SHELLNAV-26 and APPEARANCETHEME-24) rather than filed again; the stage table and the corrected root cause were posted there as a comment. |

THE SWITCH IS READ: with Team__DemoAgent__Enabled=false the log carries ZERO `Demo agent ... connected.` lines and no Room was created at startup. THE THREE EMPTY-STATE STRINGS MATCH EXACTLY. Sidebar Room list: `No rooms yet. Start an agent to create one.` Main pane: the SAME sentence, `No rooms yet. Start an agent to create one.` New chat panel: `No agents have connected yet. Start one with tools/echo-bot.ps1, or enable the demo agent in appsettings.json.` No ghost agents were listed. The main pane did NOT throw - no Blazor error (#blazor-error-ui display:none) and no error text anywhere. THE DATA DIRECTORY IS RECREATED AT STARTUP with team.db (plus -shm/-wal and an empty Teams folder), and `App_Data\rooms` did NOT exist yet at that point. AFTER `pwsh tools/echo-bot.ps1 -Name mybot`: the sidebar gained `mybot` with NO refresh (performance navigation entries still 1) while THE MAIN PANE STILL READ `No rooms yet. Start an agent to create one.` - the documented, deliberately static behaviour the Inconclusive clause says is expected and not a fail, since the auto-navigate only runs on a page load with no Room selected. Clicking the entry opened the Room with heading `mybot` and members `You, mybot`. AFTER THE FIRST MESSAGE, `App_Data\rooms\` appeared holding exactly one file, 01a0a3f3c23270a0bbd19e15a80a6a57.jsonl - so the rooms directory really is created lazily on first persistence. Reset afterwards: App_Data restored from the backup (24 files), Team__DemoAgent__Enabled cleared, app restarted on P-LAUNCH-FREE with both demo agents connecting again. |
| [PIPEEXTERNAL-36](pipe-external.md#pipeexternal-36-two-app-instances-on-one-pipe-name-split-clients-unpredictably) |  | Pass |  | THE HAZARD REPRODUCES AND THE SPLIT IS GENUINELY UNPREDICTABLE. Second instance started with Team__DataDir=App_Data2 on port 5101, same default pipe name; it started cleanly with no pipe error, and its own log shows it even took over one of the demo agents (`Created direct room ... for agent 'alpha'`) - the collision starts before any external client. FOUR NAMES, TWO INSTANCES, ALTERNATING LANDINGS: `splitter` -> 5100, `splitter1` -> 5101, `splitter2` -> 5100, `splitter3` -> 5101, read from each instance's own `Created direct room '<id>' for agent '<name>'.` line and cross-checked against each client's welcome room id. So the landings are NOT all in one window and the race is genuinely two-sided. EACH ROOM APPEARS IN EXACTLY ONE WINDOW, NEVER BOTH: the 5100 window's Room list holds `splitter` and `splitter2` and nothing else matching; the 5101 window's holds `alpha`, `splitter1`, `splitter3`. THE WINDOW THAT MISSED A CLIENT SHOWS NOTHING ABOUT IT: zero role="alert" elements in either, and neither page's text mentions the names that went to the other instance - no error, no warning, no clue, which is exactly why this gets misfiled as a delivery bug. FOUR host processes (two dotnet.exe launchers plus two Huddle.App.exe hosts), i.e. more than one. BOTH DATABASES EXIST AND EACH HOLDS ONLY ITS OWN AGENTS: querying the Users table directly, App_Data\team.db has `splitter` and `splitter2`; App_Data2\team.db has `splitter1` and `splitter3` - `splitter` is in one database only. Step 7's reply check also worked: reconnecting `splitter` landed on 5100 again with the SAME agentId, and `hi @splitter` in the 5100 window produced `splitter: **splitter:** hi splitter`. Cleaned up: the 5101 instance stopped, its window closed, and 5100 still serving. HARNESS NOTE: the second instance could not be started with a plain `dotnet run` because the first instance holds a lock on the shared build output (MSB3027 on Huddle.Contracts.dll); `dotnet run --no-build` against the already-built binaries was used instead. That is a build-output collision in this harness, not app behaviour, and it does not touch what the test measures. |
